fixed an inverted relative-transform matrix `match_clouds` inherited from the in-DCC code (2026-07-08).** With blendertk's auto_instancer port as the second consumer, the DCC-neutral halves of mayatk's `core_utils/auto_instancer` moved down to this layer (both DCCs are now thin data-extraction adapters): **(1) `PointCloud.match_clouds(points_a, points_b, tolerance, scale_tolerance, normals_a/b, normal_threshold, uvs_identical, ...)`** — the full 3-stage identity test (fast ordered max-deviation → unordered K=4-twin KDTree identity with the flip-free normal gate → centered RMS-uniform-scale normalization + robust `pca_transform` with scale fold-back and translation composition); the UV check rides as a lazy callback so it hard-rejects exactly where the in-DCC version did (stage-1/2 positional success). **(2) `PointCloud.pca_basis(points)`** — the stabilized PCA frame (third-moment sign anchors, farthest-vertex orientation of degenerate subspaces) behind transform canonicalization, and **`pca_eigenvalue_signature(points)`** — the max-normalized quantized eigenvalue bucketing descriptor. **(3) `AssemblySorter`** — the entire separated-part clustering pipeline (relative-gap area classes, vectorized same-material bbox-touch adjacency, BFS components, fractured-fusion repair, whole-multiset repeat guard, 3-model GCD count splits with touch-first growth + internal-distance-consistency assignment + distinguishability-ordered greedy fallback, orphan recovery, exemplar-distance binding, proportional-area cross-copy support gate) operating on plain part-feature dicts (contract in the module docstring; the `key`-shadowing loop variable in the distance-consistency pass was renamed in transit). **Bug fix with real-world impact:** `pca_transform`'s matrix maps cloud-b onto cloud-a (verified empirically, 3e-15), but the in-DCC composition assumed the opposite direction and only rebuilt the translation row — the relative transform placed a genuinely-rotated copy's replacement wrong whenever the rotation wasn't symmetric (R ≠ Rᵀ). It went unnoticed because the assembly flow canonicalizes first (rel = identity) and the leaf-mode tests only exercised identity/180° rotations. `match_clouds` transposes the linear block before rebuilding translation; caught by the new direction-verifying tests. **scipy now optional in robust mode:** the early `return None` gate is lifted — the existing brute-force scorer handles robust candidates (bounded memory), only the Kabsch spin refinement stays scipy-gated, and a new public `PointCloud.nn_query` helper (scipy KDTree, else chunked brute force — verified identical results) backs the unordered-identity stage (public because blendertk's geometry matcher consumes it directly — a cross-package private-API dependency would be brittle). Blender's bundled Python has no scipy; this makes the blendertk port work out of the box. Registered `AssemblySorter` in `DEFAULT_INCLUDE`/`__all__`. Tests: `test_pointcloud.py` +12 (match/reorder/count-mismatch/empty-clouds-identity/rotated-with-matrix-verification/scaled-strict-vs-scale-mode/UV-callback/half-flipped-normal-reject/no-scipy-fallback/NN-parity/basis-canonicalization-consistency/eigen-signature), new `test_assembly_sorter.py` (7 scenario mirrors of mayatk's TestAssemblySorting: stacked clasps, material bridge, one-off cluster, scaled copies, orphan recovery); full suite 1695 green. Consumer-verified: mayatk auto_instancer 55/55 + ground truth 3/3 (scene-1 precision/recall 1.000, `[5,3,1]` sharing) + scene-match 1/1 + full 3576/3576 on the delegated implementation; blendertk `test_auto_instancer.py` 38/38.
- **`Color`/`ColorPair`/`Palette` folded from a top-level `color_utils` subpackage into `core_utils/color.py` (2026-07-08).** These three are zero-dependency (`colorsys`-only) immutable value types, and despite the name every consumer uses them for UI/display theming — uitk option-box/switchboard status colors, the maya+blender hierarchy-diff tree renderers, `Palette.axes()`/`.channels()` for axis and animation-channel colors — never in the image pipeline (`img_utils`'s own color code is unrelated per-pixel sRGB/linear map math). So they belong beside the other foundational `core_utils` primitives (`NamedTupleContainer`, `SymbolRecord`), not as a top-level domain sibling to `img_utils`/`file_utils`. **Public API unchanged** — `from pythontk import Color, ColorPair, Palette` still resolves through the lazy root; only the internal module path and its one `DEFAULT_INCLUDE` registration moved (`color_utils._color_utils` → `core_utils.color`). No code anywhere imported the submodule path directly, so there was zero call-site churn across uitk/mayatk/blendertk/tentacle. Test renamed `test_color_utils.py` → `test_color.py` (one-file-per-module) and repointed to the public root import (resilient to future internal moves); full suite 1654 green.
- **`SymbolRecord` + structured `HelpMixin` output + `python -m pythontk.help` — a machine-readable introspection surface (2026-07-06).** New `pythontk/core_utils/symbol_record.py::SymbolRecord` is a frozen-shape dataclass (`name`/`qualname`/`kind`/`signature`/`summary`/`line`/`deprecated`) describing one public symbol, deliberately sized to match the fields `m3trik/scripts/generate_api_registry.py`'s static `ast`-walker already serializes into `API_REGISTRY.json` — promoting the generator's former private `SymbolEntry` onto this shared type changes zero committed bytes. `HelpMixin` now has a second, dynamic producer of the same shape: `Cls.help(as_dict=True)`/`as_json=True`, `Cls.classify(as_dict=True)`, and `Cls.list_members(as_dict=True)` return `SymbolRecord`s (enriched at the dict level with runtime-only `flags`/`defined_in`/`source` that the static side can't derive) instead of only formatted text, and `HelpMixin.about(obj, as_dict=True)` covers non-`HelpMixin` objects (modules, functions, plain classes) with `{name, signature, module, doc, source}`. The point is a common record both the committed static registry and any live class can hand an agent or RPC caller — no more scraping `help()` text. Also new `pythontk/help.py`, a shell-level introspection CLI: `python -m pythontk.help <dotted.path> [member] [--json|--source|--where|--signature|--brief]` resolves a dotted path (importing the longest importable module prefix, then walking remaining attrs) and prints the same info `HelpMixin` exposes in-process — deliberately excluded from the public registry walker (it's a CLI shell, not API surface). Tests: `test_symbol_record.py` (11), `test_help_mixin.py` (+13).
- **`HotkeyUtils` — portable Maya-style hotkey-token <-> Qt-key-sequence conversion, pulled down as the shared SSoT (2026-07-06).** New `pythontk/str_utils/hotkey_utils.py::HotkeyUtils` centralizes the on-disk hotkey-token convention (`"ctl+sht+i"`, canonical modifier order `ctl`/`alt`/`sht`, single-character keys lowercased) that both mayatk's and blendertk's `edit_utils.macros.MacroManager` were each hand-maintaining independently: `parse_key` (token -> `(ctl, alt, sht, key)`), `qt_sequence_to_key`/`key_to_qt_sequence` (round-trip against Qt's `"Ctrl+Shift+I"`-style `QKeySequence` strings), and `humanize_label` (snake_case -> Title Case for macro display names, with an acronym map and already-uppercase-word passthrough). Pure string/dict manipulation with no DCC import (no `cmds`/`bpy`), so it belongs at this DCC-agnostic layer rather than being copy-pasted twice; each `MacroManager` keeps only its own DCC-specific hotkey registration and MRO-walking macro discovery. Tests: `test_hotkey_utils.py` (13).

- **`PointCloud.pca_transform`: fixed the subsampling floor that silently broke every >`sample_size` match, plus an identity candidate and a Kabsch/ICP refinement rescue (2026-07-06).** Three robust-mode fixes, each surfaced by mayatk's AutoInstancer against a ground-truth CAD scene: **(1)** robust mode subsampled BOTH clouds independently, so a rotated query point's true twin was usually absent from the KDTree target set and even an exact copy at the exact rotation scored a nearest-neighbor floor around the inter-vertex spacing — no candidate could pass a tight tolerance for any mesh above `sample_size` (674-vertex assembly copies never matched; ≤500-vertex ones did, hiding the bug). Only the query side is subsampled now; the target stays dense. **(2)** The identity rotation is always included as a candidate: for DEGENERATE covariances (near-equal eigenvalues — a cube, a sphere) `eigh` returns an arbitrary basis per cloud, so no eigenvector-derived candidate is guaranteed to align two already-aligned clouds (a scaled duplicate in the same orientation failed to match depending on the numpy build's eigenvector choice). **(3)** New `_refine_rotation`: when no discrete candidate passes, the best coarse candidates are refined by nearest-neighbor Kabsch iterations (proper rotations only — det +1, so a reflected twin can never slip through) and re-gated at the strict tolerance + flip-free normals check; the 15°-quantized spin search around a symmetric axis can never reach a 0.001 tolerance for an arbitrary-angle copy (measured: refinement converges a rotated 674-point canister copy to mean 4e-5 with normals dot 1.0). Consumer-verified end-to-end in mayatk (5 rotated canister copies now share one shape).
- **`PointCloud.pca_transform` normals gate no longer vetoes true rigid copies over coincident-vertex twins (2026-07-05).** Hard-edged (CAD) meshes duplicate a position with different normals; the gate paired each rotated point with its single nearest neighbor, which could pick the wrong coincident twin (measured on production data: true twin at the same distance with dot=1.0 vs the picked twin at dot=-0.46) — one such phantom "flipped normal" rejected the whole candidate rotation. Each point now scores by its best-agreeing neighbor among the K=4 nearest that sit within the positional tolerance (the nearest always counts, so meshes without duplicated positions behave exactly as before, and a genuinely flipped vertex still has no agreeing twin — partial-flip rejection is unaffected). Applied to both the KDTree and brute-force scoring paths. Surfaced by mayatk's AutoInstancer failing to instance combined CAD assembly copies; behavior verified there end-to-end.
- **`SSHClient` PTY/streaming execution no longer truncates trailing output (2026-07-05).** `_execute_transport`'s read loop broke the moment `channel.exit_status_ready()` returned True, after at most one 4096-byte read per stream — but Paramiko can report the exit status while unread bytes still sit in the receive buffer, so the final chunk(s) of a command's output were silently dropped in both captured (`use_pty=True`) and streamed modes. The loop now only stops once the exit status is known AND both stdout/stderr buffers are drained. Also fixed a second latent decode defect on the same path: each chunk was decoded independently (`errors="replace"`), so a multi-byte UTF-8 character straddling a 4096-byte chunk boundary became two replacement characters; both streams now go through `codecs` incremental decoders (flushed at exit, so a genuinely truncated trailing sequence still renders as a replacement character). Regression tests: `test_net_utils.py::test_execute_transport_drains_output_buffered_at_exit` (exit status ready from the first poll with two chunks buffered — old code returned only the first) and `::test_execute_transport_multibyte_char_split_across_chunks` ("café" split mid-`é`). The plain `exec_command` path (`stdout.read()`) was never affected.
- **`ImgUtils.list_image_files` + `ImgUtils.IMAGE_EXTS` — the SfM-ingest directory-scan SSoT (2026-07-05).** The photogrammetry ingest cluster (`ExposureEqualizer` / `ImageCurator` / `MaskGenerator`) each carried an identical module-level `IMAGE_EXTS` tuple and near-identical sorted-`os.listdir` predicate — a format added in one place would silently diverge from the others. Both now live once on `ImgUtils`: `IMAGE_EXTS` (plain photographic formats — a deliberate semantic subset of the `image_formats` capability table) and `list_image_files(directory, exts=None, full_paths=False)` (sorted, case-insensitive, non-recursive; a caller-supplied `exts` accepts a bare string and any case — a raw `tuple(".png")` would have silently matched on single characters); all three modules delegate. Also routed `VidUtils.compress_video`'s `print()`-based progress/error reporting through the module logger (`logging.getLogger(__name__)`) to match the rest of the ingest cluster — a batch caller can now capture *why* a compression returned `None` instead of losing the reason to stdout. Tests: `test_img.py::ListImageFilesTest` (+4).
- **Dead-code sweep — package + test tree now pyflakes-clean (2026-07-05).** Removed unused imports/locals across 15 package modules (`class_property`, `git`, `help_mixin`, `logging_mixin`, `module_resolver`, `_gif_viewer`, the three `hierarchy_utils` modules, `metadata`, `_file_utils`, `map_optimizer`, `progression`, `ssh_client`, `_str_utils`) and 16 test files (intentional availability probes — `cv2`/`KDTree`/`pythontk.net_utils` — kept). Two of the "dead locals" were live hazards: `test_audio_utils.py` did `handler = logging.handlers = []`, clobbering the stdlib `logging.handlers` submodule attribute process-wide for every test that ran after it; and `_str_utils.truncate`'s middle-mode carried a leftover `vis` computation plus refactor-note comments. Also removed `err.txt`/`out.txt` — committed pytest-run redirects from February — and gitignored the pattern.

- **`PointCloud.pca_transform` gains optional normal-consistency verification (`normals_a`/`normals_b`/`normal_threshold`) (2026-07-05).** Point positions alone cannot distinguish a symmetric shape from a shading-variant twin — a flat plate maps onto itself under a 180° flip while its normals invert, so a purely positional best-fit can return a rotation that flips the visible faces of whatever consumes it (found via mayatk's AutoInstancer replacing symmetric CAD parts with flip-shaded instances). When both normal arrays are supplied (unit vectors paired with the point arrays), candidate rotations must also align the normals: a candidate is valid only when its point fit is within `tolerance` AND its rotated normals have zero flipped pairings (a true rigid copy aligns every normal — float noise cannot drive a ~1 dot below zero) AND mean dot ≥ `normal_threshold` (default 0.8); among valid candidates the best normal agreement wins, so a legitimately flipped symmetric twin still matches via the rotation that maps its shading too. Backward compatible — omitting the normals reproduces the previous purely positional behavior exactly. Both the KDTree path (vectorized stacked scoring) and the no-scipy brute-force fallback implement the gate. Verified: full pythontk suite 1648/1648; consumer-side coverage in mayatk's `TestAutoInstancerNormals` (partial-flip twin rejected, full-flip twin correctly matched with the shading-true rotation).
- **`PointCloud.pca_transform` rotation search vectorized + made deterministic; `ExecutionMonitor` spinner fixed for negative cursor coordinates (2026-07-05).** The robust-mode alignment scored up to 576 candidate rotations (24 bases × 24 symmetry spins) in a Python loop with one `KDTree.query` each — profiled at 31s of a 32s mayatk auto-instancing run (393k queries). All candidate rotations are now scored in a single stacked query (einsum-rotated `(K·N, 3)` batch, one C call); the no-scipy brute-force fallback keeps its per-rotation loop to bound memory. Behavior-equivalent-or-better: the old near-perfect early-exit is replaced by a global argmin over all candidates. Also replaced the `np.random.choice` subsample (matching results varied run-to-run for >500-point clouds) with deterministic stride sampling. Separately, `ExecutionMonitor._start_spinner_process` passed `--pos -122,-341` as two tokens when the cursor sits on a monitor left/above the primary — argparse reads the negative value as an option flag, the spinner exits code 2, and no indicator ever appears; now uses the `--pos=` form (regression test: `test_spinner_accepts_negative_position`, plus the previously machine-dependent-failing `test_spinner_process_start_stop` now passes cursor-anywhere).
- **Docs front door rewritten to convey the package's actual role (2026-07-04).** `docs/README.md` (the PyPI long-description and GitHub landing) now opens with a "Why" section — pythontk as the bottom of the `pythontk → uitk → mayatk/blendertk → tentacle` chain, its two organizing rules (primitives placed by data type not domain; shared code moves down to become the SSoT) — plus a package→coverage table with the lazy-root usage forms, an "Infrastructure the ecosystem is built on" section (`bootstrap_package`, preset/template stores, `AppLauncher`/`HandoffBridge`, QC gates), and a Links section matching the other ecosystem landings. The example tour was de-duplicated and every API it references verified to resolve against the installed package — which caught and fixed a stale example still calling `ptk.arrange_points_as_path`/`ptk.smooth_points` (removed in the 2026-06-19 geo_utils clean break; now `Polyline.order_points`/`Polyline.smooth`) and a leftover empty duplicate heading. Also corrected the dependency story (numpy/Pillow are hard deps per `pyproject.toml`, not optional; FFmpeg/OpenCV/rembg/PyMeshLab are the feature-gated optionals), refreshed the stale `pyproject.toml` `description` to match, replaced the hand-maintained Version badge (45 patch releases stale: said 0.8.32, actual 0.8.77 — nothing regenerates it) with the dynamic `img.shields.io/pypi/v/` shield, and fixed `test/run_tests.py::update_readme_badge` to compute the Tests-badge link target relative to the README it writes (it hardcoded `](test/)`, which would have re-broken `docs/README.md`'s `../test/` link on the next run). Follows the uitk front-door pattern (see uitk CHANGELOG 2026-07-04); pythontk stays hub-wired tier per `m3trik/docs/DOCS_STANDARD.md` (one hand-written doc — no DOCMAP ledger warranted).

- **Test-suite cleanup + two Pillow forward-compat fixes (2026-07-01).** Consolidated `test_execution_monitor*` from 4 files down to 1 (`test_execution_monitor.py`) — `test_execution_monitor_maya.py`'s 3 cases were a strict subset of `test_execution_monitor_comprehensive.py`'s, and a no-op `test_houdini` (body was just comments + `pass`) asserted nothing; the remaining, genuinely distinct coverage (DCC-python-executable resolution, spinner subprocess lifecycle, public re-export surface) was merged in as two focused `TestCase` classes, restoring the repo's "one test file per module" rule. Removed two tracked-by-mistake manual/smoke scripts with no assertions (`manual_test_execution_monitor_integration.py`, `temp_smoke_img_utils.py`) and two orphaned empty scratch dirs (`test/bench`, `test/test_output`) not referenced by any test. Dropped leftover `DEBUG:` print statements from `run_tests.py` and a module-level debug block (plus duplicate `sys`/`inspect` imports) from `test_execution_monitor.py`. Fixed two real Pillow deprecation warnings surfaced by the run: `ImgUtils.are_identical` called `Image.getdata()` (removed in Pillow 14) — switched to `np.array(image)` directly; and the `test_img.py` 16-bit-height-map fixture saved an `"I"`-mode (32-bit) image as PNG, which Pillow 13 will reject — switched to the explicit `"I;16"` mode the fixture actually means. Also dropped an unused `ImageEnhance` import from `_img_utils.py` and a dead `total` local from `run_tests.py::update_readme_badge` (pyflakes-clean now). No `temp_tests/` sweep was needed — it was already empty and correctly gitignored. Verified: full suite green before and after, `1613 passed` → `1609 passed, 18 skipped` (net -4 from removing the subset/no-op tests), 0 failures/errors/warnings.

- **`IterUtils.filter_list` gains opt-in inline negation via `negate_prefix` (2026-06-30).** A single query stream can now carry both includes and excludes: when `negate_prefix` is set (e.g. `"!"`), any *string* inclusion pattern beginning with that prefix is stripped of it and moved to the exclude set — so `inc="*keep*, !*drop*"` keeps items matching `*keep*` while dropping any matching `*drop*`. The split happens after delimiter splitting (composes with multi-pattern strings) and before the empty-filter early-exit (a query of only-negated terms still excludes); a bare marker with no remainder is dropped. Default `None` is a strict no-op — a literal leading prefix is still matched verbatim, so every existing caller is unaffected. Motivated by uitk's filter line-edit, which now exposes `!term` inline exclusion in its single-field filters (see uitk CHANGELOG 2026-06-30). Tests: `test_iter.py` (+4: inline exclude, only-negated, off-by-default literal match, bare-marker dropped).

- **`SchemaSpec` + `TemplateSet` + a pluggable `PresetStore` codec — a declarative, self-documenting template-file system (2026-06-27).** A new shared layer for "named config/template files a user can model their own after," split into three composable, Qt-free, zero-dependency primitives so the *shape* and the *storage* of a template each have one SSoT. **`SchemaSpec`** (`core_utils/schema_spec.py`) is a base for schemas *defined as a `@dataclass`*: fields declared via `spec_field(help=…, example=…, required=…, nested=…, choices=…, validate=…)` carry their own documentation, and from that single definition the base derives — with no per-schema code — `validate()` (returns a `ValidationResult` that deliberately splits **errors** (unknown discriminator, wrong structure, missing required) from **warnings** (an unrecognised key; `_`-prefixed keys are reserved for annotations and never warned), so a file that merely carries an extra key keeps working; its `raise_or_warn()` is the one-call "enforce a validated file" helper every loader shares), `skeleton()` (a fully-populated example dict to copy, omitting unset optionals), generic `from_dict`/`to_dict` (recursing nested schemas), and `describe()`/`to_markdown()` (a field-by-field reference, nested schemas rendered as sub-tables — so the doc can never drift from the enforced shape). **`PresetStore`** gained an additive `codec: Codec` parameter (default `JSON_CODEC`, fully back-compatible — every existing caller and uitk's `PresetManager` are unaffected): a `Codec(ext, load, dump)` makes the store format-agnostic, so a caller can back `*.yaml` files by injecting a YAML codec **without** pythontk taking a YAML dependency (the caller supplies it). `ext` is now a property; discovery/`path`/`load`/`save` route through the codec. **`TemplateSet`** (`core_utils/template_set.py`) is the thin glue binding a `PresetStore` (two-tier built-in + user discovery, shadowing, last-used `.active` pointer) to a `SchemaSpec` (shape): `names()`/`source()`/`user_dir`/`active`, `raw()` (plain dict) vs `load()` (parse → validate → deserialise, raising on errors, logging warnings, `strict=` to raise on warnings too), `skeleton()`, `save()`/`write_skeleton()` (write arbitrary data or the generated skeleton to the user dir), and `markdown()`. Motivated by — and first consumed by — mayatk's Shot Manifest, whose CSV *mapping* (JSON) and *behavior* (YAML) template files were each globbed from inside the installed package with no user tier, no validation, and docstring-only schemas; both now share this one mechanism (see mayatk CHANGELOG 2026-06-27). Root exports: `SchemaSpec`, `spec_field`, `ValidationResult`, `SchemaError`, `FieldDoc`, `Codec`, `JSON_CODEC`, `TemplateSet`. Tests: `test_schema_spec.py` (19: validate errors/warnings/nested/choices/custom-validator, skeleton-is-self-valid, describe/markdown, round-trip), `test_template_set.py` (10: tiering, load-validates-and-raises, raw-skips-validation, skeleton/write_skeleton, user-shadows-builtin, custom-codec ext+IO), `test_preset_store.py` (+3 codec: default-is-JSON, custom-ext round-trip, cross-codec invisibility); 45 green pythontk-side, uitk `PresetManager` suite 74 green (consumer unaffected).
- **`FileUtils.is_cloud_placeholder` + `FileUtils.free_space` — cloud/space-aware primitives for diagnosing unreadable files (2026-06-27).** Two zero-dependency, Windows-aware helpers. `is_cloud_placeholder(path)` returns True for a “dehydrated” cloud placeholder (OneDrive / Dropbox / Google Drive / Nextcloud) — it satisfies `os.path.isfile`/`os.stat` (size/mtime from placeholder metadata) but its bytes live in the cloud; reads the Windows `st_file_attributes` bitmask (OFFLINE / RECALL_ON_OPEN / RECALL_ON_DATA_ACCESS), never triggers a recall, False on non-Windows or any stat failure. **It is detection, not diagnosis:** a recall usually succeeds on demand, so a True result only means “cloud-managed, the sync client is relevant” — it does *not* imply the file is unreadable. `free_space(path)` returns the free bytes on the volume holding `path` (resolving up to the nearest existing ancestor, so it works on a file, a dir, or a not-yet-created child) via `shutil.disk_usage`, or None when no ancestor resolves; reads volume metadata only (safe on a placeholder). Together they turn an opaque `OSError` into a *checkable* cause — a (nearly) full volume, a common real reason an `open()` fails after `isfile` passed (a cloud file can’t land bytes with no room) — rather than guessing the placeholder state itself. Motivated by mayatk’s Shot Manifest, which previously mis-reported a stranded Dropbox CSV with confidently-wrong advice (“make it available offline”) when the real cause was a full partition (see mayatk CHANGELOG 2026-06-27). Tests: `test_file.py::FileTest` (+8: placeholder online-only / legacy-OFFLINE → True, local / POSIX-no-field / missing → False; free_space int for an existing dir, resolves a nonexistent child, None when unresolvable).
- **`LoggingMixin` — `setLevel` now re-enables custom levels after a level change (2026-06-27).** Fixed a latent bug where lowering a logger's level would not re-enable the custom levels (`SUCCESS`/`RESULT`/`NOTICE`). These loggers are built via the `Logger()` constructor (not `getLogger`), so they are absent from `manager.loggerDict`; the stdlib `setLevel`'s `manager._clear_cache()` therefore never touched their per-logger `isEnabledFor` cache. Once `isEnabledFor(SUCCESS)` was evaluated while the level was above `SUCCESS` (caching `False`), `success()` and the other custom levels stayed **silently** suppressed even after the level was lowered again. `LoggerExt._set_level` now clears `self._cache` directly. Surfaced by mayatk's scene_exporter promoting a per-task line to `SUCCESS`. Test: `test_logging_mixin.py::LoggerExtTest::test_setlevel_clears_custom_level_cache`; full module suite 55 green.
- **`LoggingMixin` — optional file tee + in-memory ring-buffer dump (2026-06-20).** Two opt-in ways to capture a class's log output, both off by default (zero cost until enabled), exposed as a small mixin-level API that mirrors the existing `set_log_level`. **Continuous tee:** `Cls.set_log_file(path[, level])` attaches a single managed `FileHandler` (plain text, HTML stripped) that streams every record to disk; re-calling replaces it, `set_log_file(None)` detaches+closes — a clean on/off toggle. **Buffered dump-on-demand:** `Cls.enable_log_buffer(capacity=2000)` attaches a new `RingBufferHandler` whose `emit` is O(1) and does **no** string formatting (records stored by reference, rendered only on dump), so the hot path stays cheap and the oldest record drops once capacity is exceeded; `Cls.dump_log(target=None)` renders the buffer to text and optionally writes it to a path or any writable stream (raw boxes/dividers are HTML-stripped to match the console), `clear_log_buffer()`/`disable_log_buffer()` round it out. Constructor gained `log_file=` and `log_buffer=` (True or an int capacity) alongside `log_level=`. The mechanism lives at the patched-logger layer (`LoggerExt._set_log_file`/`_enable_log_buffer`/`_dump_log`/…, registered in `_patch_logger_methods`) so any patched logger — not just `LoggingMixin` subclasses — gets the same methods; file output defaults to `NOTSET` so it captures whatever passes the logger's level. Tests: `test_logging_mixin.py::FileTeeAndBufferTest` (+9: tee/toggle/detach, buffer dump→text+file+stream, capacity eviction, resize-preserves-recent, no-buffer no-op, clear/disable, constructor wiring); full module suite 54 green.
- **`geo_utils` — new DCC-agnostic procedural-geometry subpackage; the polyline/point-cloud cluster consolidated out of `MathUtils` (2026-06-19).** Created `pythontk/geo_utils/` as the home for geometry *composed* from the `MathUtils` primitives (the layer between the math primitives and the mesh data/IO in `file_utils`; no DCC, no mesh topology). It hosts three classes, all lazy-exported at the package root:
  - **`Polyline`** — ordered polyline/curve geometry. The draped-cloth engine's `CurtainRail` was renamed here (`make`/`length`/`frames`, `length` now defaults `closed=False`, `frames` gained an optional `up` vector), and the centerline/path cluster was relocated from `MathUtils` as clean verbs: `point_at` (was `get_point_on_centerline`), `resample` (was `dist_points_along_centerline`, full signature), `from_point_cloud` (was `centerline_from_points`), `order_points` (was `arrange_points_as_path`), `smooth` (was `smooth_points`), `simplify` (RDP, was `simplify_rdp`).
  - **`PointCloud`** — unordered point-set geometry, relocated from `MathUtils`: `pca_transform` (was `get_pca_transform`), `cluster_by_distance` (was `cluster_points_by_distance`), `hash_points`.
  - **`CurtainDrape`** — moved verbatim from `math_utils.drape` (now `geo_utils.drape`); it consumes `Polyline`.
  `MathUtils` keeps the scalar/vector primitives (`lerp`/`cross_product`/`distance_between_points`/`remap`/`catenary*`/`ricker`/`smoothstep`/…) including `point_segment_distance` (the primitive `Polyline.simplify` composes). **Clean break — no back-compat aliases**: the old `MathUtils.*` / flattened `ptk.*` names are gone, and every call site across the ecosystem was migrated (mayatk `Rail(ptk.Polyline)`, tube_rig/curve_to_tube/geometry_matcher/mat_utils/xform_utils; blendertk curtain/tube_rig/tube_path/_edit_utils). No separate "tube/sweep generator" was extracted — the DCC tube builders are Maya/Blender-native ops (`extrude`/`nurbsToPoly`/bmesh) that share only the centerline/RDP geometry now living on `Polyline` (YAGNI). Tests relocated to `test_polyline.py` / `test_pointcloud.py` (+ `test_drape.py`); full suite 1541 green.
- **`core_utils.app_handoff` — general Template-Method + Strategy base for app hand-off bridges (2026-06-19).** Qt-free, DCC-free engine kit consolidating the machinery the Maya / Blender / Rizom / Substance / Marmoset bridges had each copy-pasted, designed to support **every** bridge shape via two orthogonal axes: `HandoffBridge` owns the invariant skeleton (`resolve → preflight → produce → deliver`) and knows nothing about FBX/scripts/RPC/Unity; the *deliver* step is a pluggable `Deliverer` Strategy. Per-app config is **data** — `AppSpec` (executable discovery: env/location-env/app-names/install-scan, resolved via the new `AppLauncher.resolve_app_path`/`scan_install_dirs`) and `ScriptLaunchSpec` (the shared `ScriptLaunchDeliverer`: render a `templates/*` script + launch a **fresh** target app — `--python` / `-command` / `-cfi`). `HandoffRequest`/`Payload` thread the work + produced artifacts through the skeleton. Generic script-template discovery (`core_utils.script_template`: template listing + declared-mode parsing + `__KEY__` substitution, mode-field parameterized) lives alongside. Root exports: `HandoffBridge`, `ScriptLaunchBridge`, `ScriptLaunchDeliverer`, `ScriptLaunchSpec`, `AppSpec`, `Deliverer`, `HandoffRequest`, `Payload`. Replaces the earlier short-lived `pythontk.bridge` subpackage (relocated out of the package root; the FBX-specific vocabulary generalized to "payload"). The ecosystem's bridges now express themselves through this one base: mayatk `BlenderBridge` / blendertk `MayaBridge` (ScriptLaunch), both `UnityBridge` (unitytk `CopyToAssetsDeliverer`), `MarmosetBridge` (the `MarmosetEngine` is now a `Deliverer`), `SubstanceBridge` (self-delivers — stateful RPC/instance registry). Tests: `test_bridge.py` (24).
- **`PresetStore.active` — persisted "last-selected preset" pointer (2026-06-19).** New `active` get/set property backed by a `.active` sidecar (`{"name": …}`) in the store's `user_dir`. A dotfile with no `.json` extension, so the `*.json` discovery glob ignores it (same convention as `.migrated`); `None` when unset/unreadable. `delete` clears a now-dangling pointer (kept when a built-in of the same name survives the removed user shadow); `rename` follows the active name across. This is the Qt-free SSoT for *which* preset is in use, so uitk's `PresetManager` (GUI) and any headless runner agree on the active preset across sessions. Tests: `test_preset_store.py` (+5: round-trip/cross-instance, excluded-from-listing, delete clears/keeps, rename follows).
- **`MapFactory.prepare_maps(discover_dir=...)` — gap-fill texture sets with same-base-name siblings on disk (2026-06-18).** New optional `discover_dir` arg (+ helper `_supplement_sets_from_dir`): after the input files are grouped into sets, each set is scanned against `discover_dir` for files that resolve to the *same base name* (honoring `prefix`/`suffix`) and a *recognized map type*, and any whose map type is **missing** from the set is pulled in. Provided/connected files always win — an existing map slot is never replaced and files already present aren't duplicated — so the behavior is pure additive gap-fill. Lets a caller pull in maps that live next to the inputs but were never part of the supplied list (e.g. a Normal sitting in a project's `sourceimages` that was never wired into a material). DCC-agnostic (takes a plain directory; consumers like mayatk's Material Updater resolve `sourceimages` and pass it in), and fully backward-compatible (no `discover_dir` → unchanged). Also hardened a latent aliasing issue: the `group_by_set=False` branch now copies the input into the working set (`{base_name: list(files)}`), so `prepare_maps` no longer mutates the caller's source list when `ignored_patterns` is empty (discovery appended into it). Tests: `test_map_factory.py` (+5: helper gap-fill, present-type-not-replaced, end-to-end discovery via `prepare_maps`, no-discover-dir-unchanged, no-input-mutation); full map_factory suite 43 green.
- **`MathUtils.centerline_from_points` — tube point-cloud → ordered centerline (2026-06-16).** `centerline_from_points(points, count, axis=None, precision=None) -> list[[x,y,z]]` extracts a tube mesh's centerline by slicing its vertices into slabs along the dominant (longest-bbox) axis, averaging each slab into a ring center, then resampling to exactly `count` points via the existing `dist_points_along_centerline`. Pure Python, accepts `[x,y,z]` sequences or `.x/.y/.z` objects (MPoint / bpy Vector), `[]` on degenerate input. The DCC-neutral core of blendertk's `TubePath` (and the analogue of mayatk `TubePath`'s bounding-box slicing — mayatk can adopt it too); like axis-aligned slicing it approximates a mildly-curved tube and degrades on one that doubles back along its own axis (use an explicit edge selection there). Tests: `test_math.py` (+4: straight / auto-axis / `.x.y.z`-objects / degenerate); full suite 160 green.
- **`ImgUtils.validate_image_integrity` — cheap completeness/corruption check (2026-06-16).** `validate_image_integrity(path) -> (ok, detail)` detects the files that crash native texture loaders: empty or **truncated** downloads (a partially-synced cloud file, an interrupted export) and stubs whose declared dimensions far exceed the bytes present. Pure-Python, zero-dependency, never raises: for Radiance HDR (`.hdr`/`.pic`) it parses the resolution line and walks the RLE/flat scanlines, reporting truncation precisely (e.g. "truncated: 82/4096 scanlines"); for OpenEXR it verifies the magic number + a sane minimum size; other formats only get an exists/non-empty check, and an unrecognized structure is treated as ok (never a false reject). Motivation: a partially-synced 8K `.hdr` (header said 8192×4096, only 82 scanlines on disk) was loading as a null texture in Maya's Viewport 2.0 and crashing the skydome IBL path. Tests: `test_img.py::ValidateImageIntegrityTest` (missing / empty / truncated-HDR / complete-flat-RGBE / EXR magic ±  / unknown-ext-passthrough).
- **`MathUtils.cluster_points_by_distance` — DCC-agnostic proximity flood-fill (2026-06-14).** Extracted the threshold-linked single-link spatial clustering (spatial-hash grid, ~O(N)) that backed mayatk's private `MatUtils._cluster_objects_by_distance` into a shared static helper: `cluster_points_by_distance(points, threshold) -> list[list[int]]` (clusters of indices, any dimensionality). mayatk now delegates to it (supplies bbox centres, maps indices back) and the new Blender `btk.combine_objects` (Cluster by Distance) reuses it — one SSoT instead of two copies of the flood-fill. 5 tests in `test_math.py` (two groups / transitive chain / inclusive threshold / equals naive O(N²) union-find / edge cases).

- **`MapFactory.resolve_color_space` — filename → working color space SSoT (2026-06-14).** A small additive classmethod that resolves a texture's intended color space ("sRGB" for Base Color / Albedo / Emissive, "Linear" for Normal / Roughness / Metallic and the other data maps) from its filename, reading the registry's existing per-`MapType.color_space` field — so the color/data classification has one source of truth instead of each DCC re-deriving it. DCC-agnostic: callers translate "Linear" to their own raw/data label (Maya's *Raw*, Blender's *Non-Color*). Consumed by the new `btk.fix_color_spaces`; mayatk's OCIO repair can adopt it later. Test: `test_map_factory.py::test_resolve_color_space` (color/data/unresolved-default).

- **`MatReport` — shared material/texture report formatters (2026-06-13).** The pure record→text/HTML formatting for material & texture info reports (`format_mat_info_html`/`_text`, `format_texture_info_html`/`_text`, `_fmt_size_auto`, `_path_as_link`) was extracted from mayatk's `MatUtils` into `pythontk.img_utils.mat_report.MatReport` so mayatk and blendertk share one SSoT instead of each carrying ~150 lines of identical formatting (the `CurtainDrape`/`MathUtils` pattern). The formatters are DCC-neutral — they consume a plain `{material, type, textures:[…]}` record schema produced by each DCC's `get_mat_info`. mayatk's `MatUtils.format_*` now delegate here; blendertk's new `get_mat_info` feeds the same formatter. 8 new tests in `test_mat_report.py`.

- **`MathUtils.eval_expression` + `MathUtils.convert_length_unit` — the shared Calculator engine (2026-06-13).** The safe math-expression evaluator (math-module names only, builtins disabled) and the length-unit conversion (`LENGTH_UNIT_FACTORS`: mm/cm/m/km/in/ft/yd/mi) extracted from mayatk's `CalculatorController` so the Maya and Blender calculator panels share one engine (the `CurtainDrape` pattern). mayatk's `CalculatorController.calculate`/`convert_unit` now delegate here; blendertk's new co-located calculator panel reuses it. 9 new tests in `test_math.py` (full suite 151 green).

- **`math_utils.drape` — the procedural draped-cloth (curtain) engine, extracted from mayatk (2026-06-12).** `CurtainRail` (generate / measure / resample / frame a rail polyline) + `CurtainDrape` (the pleated, catenary-sagged deformation: hang points, belly folds, V-creases, mid-fold forks, sway, billow grain) are now the ecosystem SSoT — pure geometry over the existing `MathUtils` catenary/Ricker/noise primitives, zero DCC imports. `CurtainDrape.prepare()` precomputes the per-build state; `grid_points()` yields the full draped grid so a DCC adapter only builds a mesh (mayatk `CurtainMesh` = polyPlane/MFnMesh build, blendertk `create_curtain` = bmesh). Mechanical move: mayatk's 61-test curtain suite passes unchanged against the subclassed engine. New `test_drape.py` pins the pure behavior venv-side (17 tests); full suite 1453 green.

- **Normal-map orientation convention fixed — generator + detector were label-swapped together (2026-06-11)** (`_audit/texture_pipeline_audit_2026-06-11.md`): `MapFactory.convert_bump_to_normal` emitted DirectX-orientation green under the `"opengl"` label (its own docstring said "green positive = surface pointing up"; the output faced down), and `detect_normal_map_format`'s correlation rule was flipped to match — internally consistent, so pipeline round-trips never caught it, but every bump-generated `*_Normal_OpenGL` was vertically inverted in-engine and `MapCompositor`'s declared-vs-detected advisory warned on *correct* maps. Verified three ways (first-principles derivation, the generator's own docstring, and a labeled real-world 4k OpenGL asset whose integrability correlation is -0.19). Green sign + detector rule + theory docstring corrected; regression test pins the convention against the real asset and generator↔detector agreement.
- **Second-pass audit fixes (2026-06-11).** From `_audit/second_pass_audit_2026-06-11.md`: `NamespaceHandler` placeholder resolution fixed (first item-access of a `Placeholder` raised KeyError via a double-delete; `get()` silently returned the default) and attribute access now resolves placeholders like item access (use `raw()`/`get_placeholder()` to inspect unresolved); `VidUtils.compress_video` default output derives via `splitext` and raises if output == input (the old `.replace(".avi", ".mp4")` made ffmpeg `-y` truncate any non-AVI source — data loss); `VidUtils.get_frame_rate` raises `ValueError` on unrecognized strings instead of silently returning 24.0; `MapFactory` Bump/Height→Normal conversions bind their loop var (late-binding closure made the Bump-registered converter read `inv["Height"]`); `MathUtils`: `generate_geometric_sequence` got its missing `@staticmethod`, `arrange_points_as_path` no longer empties the caller's list (works on a copy; accepts tuples), `get_two_sides_of_asa_triangle`/`xyz_rotation` use `math.pi` (results now exact), `xyz_rotation`'s dead `rotation` parameter removed, `kmeans_clustering` clamps `max_iterations` ≥ 1 (0 → NameError/empty result), `round_to_aggressive_preferred` delegates to `round_to_preferred(max_distance=10)` (~100 duplicated lines removed); `AudioUtils` imports numpy lazily (zero-dep rule — module no longer requires numpy at import). Regression tests added in `test_namespace_handler/vid/math/map_factory`; suite 1432 green.
- **Audit fix pass — correctness P0s (2026-06-11).** Live-verified bugs from the 2026-06-11 audit (`_audit/pythontk_audit_2026-06-11.md`): `CoreUtils.set_attributes` no longer silently drops falsy values; `are_similar` returns False on length mismatch instead of zip-truncating; `cycle(query=True)` is now read-only (no state creation/advance on unknown keys); `IterUtils.nested_depth` passes `typ` through recursion; `make_iterable` dropped the dead PyMel `__apimfn__` marker check; `StrUtils.get_trailing_integers(as_string=True)` returns None (not `"None"`) on no match; `time_stamp` attach/detach now round-trips and survives paths with spaces; `FileUtils.format_path` preserves drive roots (`"C:/"` no longer becomes CWD-relative `"C:"`); broken-by-design `FileUtils.get_file` (returned a closed handle) deleted; `ImgUtils.create_mask` fixed (transposed corner indexing crashed non-square images; scalar-bool indexing) ; `pack_channels(grayscale_to_rgb=True)` replication branch was unreachable — now works; `generate_mipmaps` returns the actual mip chain instead of discarding it (dead `MapOptimizer` mipmaps plumbing removed — PIL can't embed mips); `convert_rgb_to_hsv` delegates to PIL (old per-pixel loop crashed with OverflowError on hues > 255°); `convert_rgb_to_gray` str-path fixed; `convert_i_to_l` scales 16-bit instead of truncating modulo 256; `SingletonMixin` keys instances per `(class, singleton_key)` (cross-class collisions returned the wrong class) and consumes `singleton_key` before `__init__` (TypeError); **`HelpMixin.mro()` renamed `show_mro()`** — it shadowed Python's built-in `type.mro()` on every subclass ecosystem-wide; logger styling is now explicit-kwargs only (`preset=`/`color=`) — the positional heuristic swallowed `%s` substitution when an arg matched a preset/color name; missing `@staticmethod` added to `parse_method_args`/`filter_results`. Regression tests added across `test_core/iter/str/file/img/singleton_mixin/help_mixin/logging_mixin`; suite 1443 green.

- **`ImgUtils.compute_atlas_layout` + `assemble_atlas` — texture-atlas layout & compositing primitives (for lightmap atlasing).** Two DCC-agnostic pieces that turn N weighted items into one packed atlas. **`compute_atlas_layout(weights, rows=None)`** is a deterministic shelf packer: it returns one `(scaleX, scaleY, offsetX, offsetY)` rect per item that **tiles the unit square exactly** (no gaps/overlaps), with each rect's *area* proportional to its weight so a large object gets more atlas texels — exactly the `scaleOffset` form an atlas needs and Unity's `Renderer.lightmapScaleOffset` convention (`uv' = uv*scale + offset`). Items are balanced across `round(√n)` shelves by longest-processing-time to keep aspect ratios sane; all-zero weights fall back to equal shares, negatives clamp to 0. Pure Python (no numpy/PIL). **`assemble_atlas(images, rects, size, background=0)`** composites each item's image into its rect on one canvas, resizing (cv2; `INTER_AREA` downscale) and applying the standard UV→row **vertical flip** (UV v=0 bottom ↔ image row 0 top) so an item later bound with the same `scaleOffset` samples the pixels written; HDR-safe (float32), channel/dtype preserved, degenerate (zero-area) rects skipped. Consumed by mayatk's `LightmapBaker.pack_atlas` (Atlas-by-Material packing). Tests: `test/test_img.py::AtlasLayoutTest` (exact tiling, area∝weight, order preserved, zero-weight-shelf guard, n up to 37) + `AtlasAssembleTest` (UV-flip placement, dtype/size, zero-rect skip, grayscale 2D round-trip) — +15, full img suite 121 green.
- **Per-map output-format templates — save each map in its target profile's exact container + bit depth.** New `OutputSpec(ext, bit_depth, compression)` + `OutputTemplate` (`img_utils/output_template.py`), with the built-in per-`WF`-profile catalogue and resolution encapsulated in an `OutputTemplates` registry class (`OutputTemplates.resolve(map_type, profile)` / `.get(profile)` / `.BUILTIN` / `.DEFAULT`); plain-data (`to_dict`/`from_dict`) so a future editable `PresetStore` tier can wrap them as the built-in tier. `ImgUtils.save_image` gained **`bit_depth=`** (16-bit PNG/TIFF — grayscale via PIL `I;16`, RGB via cv2 `uint16`; 8-bit sources promoted ×257; containers that can't hold 16-bit degrade to 8-bit + warning) and **`compression=`** for DDS block formats (`DXT1/DXT3/DXT5/BC5` via Pillow now; `BC7/BC6H` route to an optional codec registered through `ImgUtils.register_dds_codec`, raising a clear error until an extension installs one). The pipeline applies a profile's template **only when `output_profile` is set** — `TextureProcessor.save_map` (the per-map save chokepoint), `MapOptimizer.optimize_map`, and `MapCompositor` each resolve per-map ext/bit-depth/compression from it; behavior is **unchanged when unset** (fully backward-compatible). This is the engine/SSoT layer (built-in tier); editable templates + panel profile selectors are the follow-up. Tests: `test/test_output_template.py` (resolution: per-profile, override/default fallback, unknown profile/map, dict round-trip) + `test/test_img.py` (16-bit grayscale/RGB round-trips, unsupported-container fallback, DXT5, BC7 clear-error + codec hook).
- **`ImgUtils` image-format capability table + DDS support — one SSoT for IO routing.** Replaced the flat `texture_file_types` list with `image_formats` (`{ext: ImageFormat(read, write, backend)}`) and *derived* `recognized` / `readable` / `writable` from it so the sets can't drift; `texture_file_types` remains as a back-compat alias of `recognized`. `save_image` / `load_image` now dispatch on each format's `backend` — Pillow for most, OpenCV for the float formats (EXR, HDR) Pillow can't encode/decode — via `_save_via_cv2` / `_load_via_cv2`; a read-only or unknown format raises a clear `ValueError` instead of a cryptic `KeyError`. **DDS** is now a recognized read+write format (Pillow's DXT-tier writer; BC7/BC6H still raise loudly). Every previously divergent writer was unified through `save_image` — `MapOptimizer.optimize_map`, the seven direct `Image.save` calls in `MapFactory`, and `ImgUtils.pack_channels` / `extract_channels` — so EXR/HDR/DDS output works everywhere and there is a single save path. `OPENCV_IO_ENABLE_OPENEXR` is set at module import (OpenCV caches it at first codec init). Consumed by mayatk (`GameShader`, `MaterialUpdater`) and extapps (`map_converter`), which now build their convert-format menus from `writable`. Tests: `test/test_img.py::ImageFormatCapabilityTest` (table/derived-set integrity, alias preserved, DDS+EXR+HDR round-trips, read-only-format guard, `get_images` survives an EXR in the scanned dir).
- **`ImgUtils.dilate_image` — texture edge-padding / gutter fill (pure numpy, HDR-safe).** `dilate_image(image, mask=None, iterations=-1, connectivity=)` extends valid pixels outward into empty (background) regions so bilinear filtering and mip generation never pull background color across a UV-island seam — the standard "dilation" / "edge padding" baking tools apply. Each pass assigns every empty pixel adjacent to filled pixels the average of its filled neighbors (`iterations=-1` fills entirely; a bounded count gives a fixed gutter width); numpy-in/numpy-out, same shape/dtype, no PIL/cv2 dependency so it works on float HDR data. The optional `mask` is the key contract: it defaults to "any channel > 0", but baked-map callers must pass the explicit **coverage** mask — a luminance heuristic wrongly treats dark-but-valid texels (shadow contact, near-black albedo) as empty and overwrites them. Consumed by mayatk's `LightmapBaker` (mask = Arnold RTT alpha). Tests: `test/test_img.py::DilateImageTest` (single-source fill; dark-valid-pixel preserved as a fill source under an explicit mask vs. overwritten under the default; iteration bound; shape/dtype/2D; all-empty terminates; mask-shape mismatch raises).
- **`MathUtils.catenary_sag` — added a `gather` push-pull lobe at the supports.** The existing `round_amount` only *eases* the cusp into a smooth dome (it reduces sag near each support but never rises above the baseline or dips back). The new `gather` arg (default `0`, orthogonal to `round_amount`) adds the missing overshoot: the `ring²·(2.4 − 3.4·ring)` lobe (`ring = cos²(πs)`) lifts the profile **above** the baseline right at the support (−1 sag-unit at `gather=1` — a gathered-header pucker rising above the rail) and **dips** just inside it (`+0.18`) as the slack falls off, easing back to `0` by mid-span so the center peak is untouched. Consumed by mayatk's Curtain generator as its new push-pull *Gather* dial. Tests: `test/test_math.py` (push-above-baseline at the support, pull-extra-sag inside, center-untouched, and `gather=0` matches the plain catenary).
- **`MathUtils.simplify_rdp` + `point_segment_distance` — curve simplification / adaptive sampling.** **`simplify_rdp(points, tolerance)`** is the Ramer-Douglas-Peucker algorithm: it returns the indices of the polyline vertices to *keep* so the simplified polyline stays within `tolerance` of the original, concentrating the kept points where the polyline bends (corners, tight arcs) and collapsing near-collinear runs to their endpoints — the standard, error-bounded way to decide *where to spend* samples/divisions along a curve. Iterative (explicit stack, safe on dense polylines), any fixed dimension, monotone under tolerance, endpoints always retained; `tolerance <= 0` or `< 3` points returns everything. Backed by **`point_segment_distance(p, a, b)`** (perpendicular distance to a segment, endpoint-clamped, n-dimensional) — a reusable primitive alongside the existing point/polyline helpers. Consumed by mayatk's Curve to Tube to place polygon rings on the bends of the path. Tests: `test/test_math.py` (segment distance perpendicular/clamped/degenerate; RDP collapses straights, keeps corners, concentrates on bends, 2D, edge cases).
- **Math/signal primitives promoted from the mayatk Curtain generator.** Reusable, DCC-agnostic pieces that were private to `mayatk.edit_utils.curtain` now live at the SSoT layer. `MathUtils` gains: **`smoothstep(x, edge0=0, edge1=1)`** (the canonical *clamped* Hermite — distinct from `ProgressionCurves.smooth_step`, which applies a weight exponent and doesn't clamp), **`ricker(x)`** (Mexican-hat wavelet — a zero-integral ridge-with-troughs, for mean-preserving bumps/creases/folds), **`catenary(t, tension)`** + **`catenary_sag(t, tension, round_amount=0)`** (normalized `cosh` sag profile for cables/ropes/cloth, optionally rounded toward `sin²` at the supports), **`safe_normalize(vector, fallback)`** (zero-guarded `normalize`), and **`lerp`** now interpolates point/vector sequences component-wise in addition to scalars. New **`BandLimitedNoise`** (`math_utils/noise.py`, exported at top level): coherent multi-octave sine noise (1/f roll-off, deterministic from `seed`, optional `u_periodic` seam-wrap) — the opposite of per-vertex white noise, for procedural surface relief (cloth grain, gentle terrain, displacement). Tests: `test/test_math.py` (the new MathUtils helpers) and `test/test_noise.py` (determinism, coherence, amplitude bound, periodic wrap).
- **`PresetStore` — Qt-free, two-tier named-preset store (built-in + user).** Where `UserConfig` resolves a *single* config doc, `PresetStore` manages a *collection* of named JSON presets across a read-only **built-in** tier (shipped in a repo `presets/` dir next to a module) and a writable **user** tier under `user_config_root()` (the same consolidated root uitk's `PresetManager` uses). A user preset **shadows** the built-in of the same name (replace, not merge — "duplicate to edit" covers tweaking a shipped default), so `list()` shows each name once and `load()` returns the user copy when present. API: `list(tier=None|"user"|"builtin")`, `source(name) → "user"|"builtin"|None`, `exists`, `load` (KeyError on missing), and `save`/`delete`/`rename` operating on the user tier only (built-ins stay read-only); `sanitize_preset_name` is the shared name→filename rule. It deals only in plain dicts and imports no Qt, so headless engines (e.g. the photogrammetry runners in Metashape's bundled Python 3.9) use it directly — and uitk's `PresetManager` now delegates its storage/discovery to it, so the GUI and headless paths share one set of dirs, names, and rules. Exposed at the top level (`pythontk.PresetStore`, `pythontk.sanitize_preset_name`). Tests: `test/test_preset_store.py` (tier union + user-shadowing, source/exists, load-prefers-user + KeyError-on-missing + available-list, save/delete/rename user-only with built-in read-only protection, shared name sanitization, default user dir under `user_config_root`).
- **Marmoset Toolbag engine removed from `pythontk` (`marmoset_utils` deleted).** `pythontk` is the zero-dependency, DCC-agnostic core; a Toolbag-SDK integration engine is domain glue, not a generic primitive — it was an over-centralization (see the earlier "Marmoset Toolbag engine" entry below, now superseded). `MarmosetEngine` is no longer exported at the top level. The engine + `templates/` + `_toolbag_helpers` + `toolbag_log` + `template_params` + the `marmoset_rpc` live-session client now live with each consumer: `mayatk.mat_utils.marmoset_bridge` (full, incl. `marmoset_rpc`) and the standalone `extapps.marmoset_workflow` panel (engine subset — `import`/`lookdev`, no bake, no RPC). The genuinely-generic primitives it builds on stay upstream and unchanged: `pythontk.net_utils.rpc.RpcClient` (JSON-RPC base) and `uitk.bridge` (param widgets + `__KEY__` template rendering). Mirrors how `substance_bridge` / `substance_workflow` are already handled. The mock tests moved to `mayatk/test/mock_tests/` (`test_toolbag_helpers.py`, `test_marmoset_rpc.py`).
- **`ImgUtils.get_image_size(path)` — zero-dependency image dimensions.** Reads `(width, height)` straight from the JPEG/PNG header with the **stdlib only** (a `_image_size_from_header` parser: PNG IHDR + JPEG SOF scan, guarded against a malformed-segment loop), falling back to PIL for other formats when available. Complements `get_image_info` (PIL-based) for the case where you need only the dimensions and **can't assume PIL/cv2/numpy** — notably Metashape's bundled Python, where `extapps`' `--texture-size auto` was silently falling back to its 8192 default instead of the source's true resolution. Test: `test/test_img.py::ImgTest::test_get_image_size_header_parse` (non-square PNG+JPEG; garbage → `None`).
- **`ExposureEqualizer` — near-lossless, EXIF-preserving output (was silently degrading the set).** The equalizer re-saved every frame via `cv2.imwrite` at its default JPEG quality (~95) with chroma subsampling and **no EXIF** — so an equalized set fed to alignment carried generational re-compression artifacts (which hurt SfM feature matching) and had lost its focal-length / camera priors. `equalize_directories` now takes `quality` (default `100`) and `preserve_exif` (default `True`); JPEG output is a single high-quality 4:4:4 encode that carries the source EXIF through (via PIL when available, else a `cv2` high-quality fallback), and PNG/TIFF stay lossless. Backward-compatible (new kwargs default to the safer behavior). Frames are read with `IMREAD_IGNORE_ORIENTATION` so the saved pixels stay in the same frame as the copied `Orientation` tag — otherwise cv2's default auto-righting + the verbatim tag would double-rotate the image in any EXIF-aware consumer (RealityScan / Metashape). Docstring now notes equalization's payoff is seam-free texturing across **multiple** captures — for *alignment*, prefer leaving radiometry to the SfM tool. Test: `test/test_vid.py::ExposureEqualizerTest::test_equalize_preserves_exif`.
- **`ImageCurator` — median-relative blur guard (`min_sharpness_fraction_of_median`).** The percentile floor only removes a fixed bottom slice of the survivor set, so when a capture has more blur than that slice, boundary-case *catastrophically* defocused frames slip through (found in testing: a frame at ~0.3% of the set median survived). The new guard (`curate`/`preview`, 0–1, default `0.0` = off) additionally drops any cluster representative below that fraction of the **survivor-median** sharpness — self-calibrating, near-dup blur can't dilute it, and the strictest of the three floors wins. Off by default so existing consumers are unchanged; the photogrammetry workflows enable it at `0.15`. New test in `test/test_vid.py` (`test_median_fraction_guard_drops_catastrophic_blur`).
- **`UserConfig` — Qt-free, zero-dependency user-config resolution (`core_utils/user_config.py`).** Lets a package ship a generic default + an `*.example.json` template while the user's personal/site values live in a JSON file **outside** the package source. `UserConfig.resolve(name, package=, env=, default=, path=)` discovers the source file (explicit `path` → `$env` → `<user_config_root>/<package>/<name>.json` → none) and **deep-merges** the user doc over the default, so a partial profile need only carry overrides; `expand()` resolves `~` / `${ENV}` in path values. `user_config_root()` reproduces uitk's consolidated config location (`%LOCALAPPDATA%/uitk`, `~/.config/uitk`, `~/Library/Preferences/uitk`; override `UITK_PRESETS_ROOT`) **without importing Qt** — uitk's `SettingsManager`/`PresetManager` are `qtpy`-bound and unusable from headless contexts (e.g. the photogrammetry engines inside Metashape's bundled Python 3.9), so this honors the same root *by convention* and the two stay in lockstep without a dependency. JSON, not TOML (`tomllib` is 3.11+; won't import under 3.9). Used by `extapps.photogrammetry_profile`. Tests: `test/test_user_config.py`.
- **`AppLauncher` — interactive-session launch + detection (for headless DCC/SDK driving).** New `current_session_id()` / `active_console_session_id()` / `is_interactive_session()` (Windows session awareness — session 0 is the non-interactive services session with no window station) and `launch_in_session(app, args, session=None, ...)`, which launches a window-station-bound app into the **active console session** from a non-interactive context (SSH / service session 0) via Sysinternals PsExec. PsExec is *discovered* (`find_session_launcher`: explicit → `$PSEXEC` → PATH → tool dirs), never bundled — pythontk stays zero-dependency. `run()` also gains additive `output_file` (stream stdout+stderr to a file instead of buffering huge logs in memory) and `env`. All additions are backward-compatible. Used by the new `extapps` Metashape/RealityScan launch connections. Tests in `test/test_app_launcher.py` (`TestAppLauncherSessions`).
- **`ImageCurator.preview()` — dry-run curation report.** Scans a set **once** and evaluates one or more dHash `hash_thresholds` *without copying any files*, returning `{n_scanned, sharpness:{min,p5,p25,median,p75,max}, thresholds:[{hash_threshold, n_clusters, n_kept, reduction_pct, sharpness_floor}]}` — so curation can be tuned on a real capture before committing a long SfM run. The scan / cluster / select stages were extracted into shared `_scan_images` / `_cluster` / `_select` helpers (DRY — `curate()` and `preview()` now share one implementation; no behavior change to `curate()`). Test: `ImageCuratorTest.test_preview_reports_survivors_without_copying`.
- **Photogrammetry prep — sharper, portable culling + gentler equalization.** (1) `ImageCurator.curate()` gains `sharpness_floor_percentile` (0–100): derives the blur cutoff from the variance-of-Laplacian distribution of the **cluster representatives** (the sharpest-per-cluster frames actually headed downstream) instead of a scene-dependent absolute constant, so the same setting drops "the blurriest ~10%" of the survivor set on any capture. Computing it over representatives rather than every scanned frame is deliberate — a large mass of blurry near-duplicates (e.g. a handheld floor-pan segment) would otherwise drag an all-frames cutoff down and under-cull. Applied after per-cluster top-K selection, so only uniformly-blurry clusters are culled; parallax-bearing sharp views survive. (2) `ExposureEqualizer.equalize_directories()` gains `strength` (0–1, blends the Reinhard match with each frame's original in LAB so local contrast isn't flattened — right before tools that re-balance internally, e.g. RealityCapture MosaicBlending) and `reference_strategy` (`first`/`median`/`global`) so the target distribution isn't forced to the first capture's color cast (`median` picks the dir nearest the set-wide mean luma; `global` averages all). Defaults preserve prior behavior (`strength=1.0`, `reference_strategy="first"`). New tests in `test/test_vid.py` (`ImageCuratorTest`, `ExposureEqualizerTest`).
- **Marmoset Toolbag engine** (`marmoset_utils`) — the DCC-agnostic Toolbag integration carved out of `mayatk.mat_utils.marmoset_bridge`. `MarmosetEngine` launches Toolbag and runs a rendered template (`send_to` interactive / `roundtrip` headless) against a plain **model path** + optional materials-manifest + plain params dict, so any host composes it (the Maya bridge, the new `extapps.marmoset_workflow` panel, a CLI). Ships `toolbag_log` (log resolution/classification/tail), `template_params` (UI-free default token values), the in-Toolbag `_toolbag_helpers`, the `templates/` scripts, and the `marmoset_rpc` live-session client (a `RpcClient` binding). `render_template`'s first path kwarg is now `model_path` (was `fbx_path`); the template token is `__MODEL_PATH__`. `pythontk.MarmosetEngine` is exposed at the top level. Moved-code tests live in `pythontk/test/` (`test_toolbag_helpers.py`, `test_marmoset_rpc.py`; 96 pass).
- **Python 3.9 compatibility restored** (matches the declared `requires-python = ">=3.9"`). Four modules used PEP 604 unions (`X | Y`) in annotations without `from __future__ import annotations`, which raises `TypeError` at import time on 3.9 — breaking hosts that ship a 3.9 interpreter (notably Agisoft Metashape, whose bundled Python runs the `extapps` photogrammetry engines). Added the future import to `net_utils/credentials.py`, `core_utils/logging_mixin.py`, `core_utils/execution_monitor/_dialog_viewer.py`, and `core_utils/execution_monitor/_execution_monitor.py`. New `test/test_py39_compat.py` statically enforces the contract (PEP 604 annotations require the future import; no runtime unions; no `match` statements) so it can't silently regress. Verified by importing all 78 modules under Metashape's bundled Python 3.9.13.
- **Structural consolidation (no public-API change).** Reduced localized bloat without touching the public surface (`from pythontk import ...` and module paths for `MapFactory`/`TextureProcessor`/`ConversionRegistry`/`HierarchyDiff` resolve as before; full suite green):
  - `img_utils/map_factory.py` (4,428 lines) split into a `map_factory/` package — `conversions` (registry) → `processor` (`TextureProcessor`) → `handlers` (workflow strategies) → `_map_factory` (orchestrator). `MapFactory` is late-bound into `processor`/`handlers` by the package `__init__` to break the runtime cycle their primitive calls would otherwise create.
  - `core_utils/hierarchy_diff.py` moved into the `hierarchy_utils/` subpackage so all hierarchy code lives together. `HierarchyDiff` (aggregate result container) and `HierarchyAnalyzer`/`HierarchyDifference` (engine + per-record) are complementary, not duplicates, so they were **not** merged.
  - Fixed dead `DEFAULT_INCLUDE` entry `core_utils.table_mixin` → `TableMixin` (the class lives in `logging_mixin`); `pythontk.TableMixin` now resolves instead of returning nothing.
  - Removed dead code: orphan `file_utils/mesh_convert/mesh_convert_ui.py` (unreferenced PySide6 import — violated the zero-Qt rule) and the unused `core_utils/hierarchy_utils/backup/` snapshot.
- **`TextureOptimizer` → `MapOptimizer`** (breaking) — renamed `img_utils/texture_optimizer.py` → `img_utils/map_optimizer.py`, class `TextureOptimizer` → `MapOptimizer`, methods `optimize_texture` → `optimize_map`, `batch_optimize_textures` → `batch_optimize_maps`. Brings the module into line with its `map_*` siblings (`map_compositor`, `map_factory`, `map_registry`). No back-compat shim — downstream callers (`mayatk`, `extapps`, `uitk` docstrings) updated in lockstep.
- **Removed `ImgUtils.map_modes` / `ImgUtils.map_types` / `ImgUtils.map_backgrounds`** (breaking) — these registry-derived class attrs invited a backwards `_img_utils → map_registry` module-load coupling. `MapRegistry` is the sole source of truth; access via `MapRegistry().get_map_modes()` etc. (singleton, cheap). `ImgUtils.set_bit_depth` and `ImgUtils.get_base_texture_name` use a deferred local import so generic `ImgUtils` consumers no longer pay the map-registry instantiation cost on import.
- **MapCompositor engine** (`img_utils.map_compositor`) — multi-layer texture compositor with auto DirectX/OpenGL normal-map complement generation. Moved here from the standalone `map_compositor` repo; the UI panel now ships in `extapps`.
- **FrameExtractor** (`vid_utils.frame_extractor`) — OpenCV-backed video → still-frame extraction. Carved out of the deprecated `metashape_workflow` repo as a generic helper.
- **UI panels removed** (clean cut, no shims): `map_converter`, `map_packer`, `mesh_convert` Switchboard slot classes / `.ui` files have moved to `extapps`. Engine logic (`ImgUtils`, `MapFactory`, `MeshConvert`) stays here. `pythontk.MeshConvertSlots` and `pythontk.MeshConvertUi` no longer exist at the top level — import the panel from `extapps.mesh_convert` if needed.
- **Pillow** added as a declared dependency (already used transitively by `ImgUtils`, `MapFactory`, `MeshConvert`, and `_gif_viewer`; declaration was overdue).
- **Affix hardening** — `StrUtils.strip_known_affix` / `StrUtils.apply_affix` idempotently strip and reapply a configured prefix/suffix (case-insensitive, separator-tolerant including stray leading/trailing `_`, no false-positives like `Matte_door` for prefix `Mat_`). `strip_known_affix` is a conservative primitive — it touches only the affix and adjacent separators, never internal/remote underscores. `apply_affix` is a no-op when both affixes are empty (so user-typed names pass through untouched), and trims dangling underscores only on the side(s) where an affix is being applied. `ImgUtils.get_base_texture_name`, `MapFactory.get_base_texture_name`, `MapFactory.group_textures_by_set`, `MapFactory.resolve_texture_filename`, and `MapFactory.prepare_maps` now accept `prefix`/`suffix` and route them through, so callers can safely round-trip texture/material names without producing `Mat_Mat_<name>` duplicates or trailing-underscore artifacts.
- **AudioUtils module** — reusable ffmpeg-backed audio conversion helpers (`ensure_playable_path`, extension checks, ffmpeg resolution) for downstream DCC tools.
- **Audio Composite Builder** — DCC-agnostic WAV compositing helper (`build_composite_wav`) — centralizes timeline-audio mixing logic.
